# Orion — Decision Log

One line per decision. Newest last.

⚠ **This ledger is checked by `tools/check-decisions.py`**, which fails on a
duplicate number, on a number cited by the tree that has no row, and on a gap
that is not declared below. Run it with the other gates. It exists because the
queue in `STATUS.md` offered **already-shipped work as the next story three
times**, and the third time the reason was that #112 — the decision that closed
the queue item — had never been written down at all.

⚠ **Declared gaps: 22, 23, 24.** No row was ever written for any of them —
checked with `git log -S` over this file's whole history — and **nothing in the
tree cites them**, in code or in prose. They are numbers that were skipped on
2026-07-27, not decisions that were lost, and there is nothing to recover. Left
as gaps rather than backfilled with something invented, and listed here so the
check above can tell a skipped number from a missing one.

⚠ **`71b`** is a second decision written on 2026-07-30 into a number already
taken by the wordmark row. Nothing cited it, and the wordmark's `#71` is cited
once, so the uncited one took the letter — the number is an identifier, not a
sort key, and its position in the table still carries the date.

⚠ **The header used to warn that two rows were numbered 96** — the
highlight-reconstruction method and the creative vignette — and said twelve
files cite "decision #96" and every one means the vignette. **All three claims
are false as of 2026-08-02.** There is **one** row 96 and it is the highlight
reconstruction; there is **one** citation of #96 in the tree,
`engine/shaders/hl_mask.slang:110`, and it means region scoping, which is that
row. The twelve were `.claude/worktrees/` copies of one file counted as twelve.
The duplicate that really existed was **71**, which the warning never mentioned,
and it sat under a warning about the wrong number for two days.

| # | Date | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 2026-07-27 | Planning docs live in `planning/`, kept concise | Single source of truth for Opus build sessions |
| 2 | 2026-07-27 | macOS first; Windows/Linux later | Smaller test matrix, native Metal + Core ML, ship sooner |
| 3 | 2026-07-27 | **No Rust.** Engine in C++20 | User can't debug Rust. C++ also has the stronger imaging ecosystem (LibRaw, OCIO, lensfun, lcms2) |
| 4 | 2026-07-27 | GPU via Metal; shaders authored in Slang | Best Mac tooling + f16 + ANE path; Slang keeps SPIR-V/HLSL portability open |
| 5 | 2026-07-27 | Engine = compute DAG, one shader per node, `rgba16f` linear Rec.2020 | vkdt's proven design; avoids darktable's dual CPU/OpenCL maintenance tax |
| 6 | 2026-07-27 | Scene-referred pipeline, exactly one display transform, no Lab | Avoids artifacts/hue shifts of display-referred; industry direction |
| 7 | 2026-07-27 | Default tone mapper: AgX-family sigmoid, 2–3 sliders | Great defaults without filmic's fiddly multi-tab UI |
| 8 | 2026-07-27 | Demosaic: RCD default (MIT impl), bilinear for thumbs | Near-AMaZE quality at PPG speed, excellent GPU fit |
| 9 | 2026-07-27 | XMP sidecars = source of truth; SQLite = disposable rebuildable index | Folder-first workflow, no catalog lock-in, but instant filtering on large shoots |
| 10 | 2026-07-27 | Avoid exiv2 (GPL-2.0); use Apple ImageIO for metadata | Prevents forced open-sourcing / commercial license fee |
| 11 | 2026-07-27 | Sony ARW is the only priority format for v1 | User's camera; dogfooding beats breadth |
| 12 | 2026-07-27 | Engine is a library with a narrow C API; UI is a client | Makes UI choice reversible and Windows port tractable |
| 13 | 2026-07-27 | **No Vulkan.** Metal only on macOS | Vulkan needs ~1000 lines of boilerplate to draw a triangle; unmaintainable for a solo dev |
| 14 | 2026-07-27 | Maintainability is a hard constraint: one node = one 50–150 line shader; adding a feature = 3-file change | Solo developer must be able to hand-edit any file months later |
| 15 | 2026-07-27 | Build bilateral grid + Bilateral Guided Upsampling as first-class DAG node types in M1 | General escape hatch for the 16 ms budget — decouples algorithm cost from slider responsiveness (MIT/Google, open-source impl) |
| 16 | 2026-07-27 | `CLAUDE.md` at root + `planning/STATUS.md` updated every session | Survives context clears and session wipes |
| 17 | 2026-07-27 | **No bespoke/Skia UI layer** | 1.5–2.5 engineer-years for macOS alone; Zed took 5 yrs + 1M LOC funded. Impossible solo |
| 18 | 2026-07-27 | No Dear ImGui for product UI — but **use it for internal debug tooling** | Its own FAQ says skinning is limited, built for debug tools. Perfect for a pipeline inspector |
| 19 | ~~2026-07-27~~ | ~~UI shell = SwiftUI/AppKit~~ **SUPERSEDED by #21** | Was based on two errors: HDR is not Qt-exclusive-missing, and the platform-integration argument killed bespoke, not Qt |
| 20 | ~~2026-07-27~~ | ~~`@Observable` view models~~ **VOID** — SwiftUI not chosen | |
| 21 | ~~2026-07-27~~ | ~~UI shell = Qt 6 / QML~~ **SUPERSEDED by #25** | Was correct when the criterion was "stay in one language". Priorities shifted to stunning look + minimal bloat, where native wins |
| 22–24 | ~~2026-07-27~~ | ~~Qt LGPL / styling rules~~ **VOID** — Qt not chosen | Note for the record: Qt is **not** paywalled. LGPLv3 is genuinely free for closed-source commercial use with dynamic linking |
| 25 | 2026-07-27 | **UI shell = SwiftUI/AppKit + C++ engine** (final) | Stunning look and minimal bloat are the top two priorities; native wins hardest on both. ~5–15 MB bundle vs Qt's 40–60 MB; Apple materials/vibrancy/typography free vs building a visual language from scratch. Zero licensing obligations |
| 26 | 2026-07-27 | View models = plain `@Observable` objects, zero SwiftUI types inside | Any panel re-hostable in AppKit when SwiftUI hits a density cliff. Cheap on day one, expensive to retrofit. Sets macOS 14+ floor |
| 27 | 2026-07-27 | Narrow hand-authored POD facade at the C++↔Swift boundary | No templates, no move-only types, **no exceptions escaping (they terminate the process)**. Only boundary stable across Swift releases |
| 28 | 2026-07-27 | Rejected: C++ webview shells (`webview/webview`, saucer), WebGPU, WebAssembly, Go | Webview canvas layering is fragile and the canvas *is* the app. WebGPU is an abstraction over Metal (no speedup). Wasm is slower. Go has no imaging libraries, cgo overhead, GC in the pixel path |
| 29 | 2026-07-28 | **Clip the mosaic to a single white level after white balance**, in `linearize`, before demosaic | A blown pixel arrives as (S, S, S) and white balance turns it into the gains — magenta on every clipped light. dcraw's default highlight mode; before demosaic because RCD interpolates across an unclipped neighbour and leaves a fringe rather than a clean edge. Costs the headroom highlight reconstruction could have used, which is the right trade: the cast was on every frame, the reconstruction is optional |
| 30 | 2026-07-28 | **A control's readout is its own reset**, colored by whether it has moved | One affordance says which controls are modified *and* what is clickable. A separate change dot in the margin would say it twice, smaller and harder to hit. The base is per photo, not per type: white balance is the camera's reading |
| 31 | 2026-07-28 | **Export converts through ColorSync, tagged sRGB at source** | A chromatic adaptation typed in here is a cast waiting to happen, and ColorSync's definitions are what every other application reads the file against. Tagging the pixels as the destination instead of converting them is how a file opens oversaturated |
| 32 | 2026-07-28 | **Export metadata comes from ImageIO reading the RAW**, not from LibRaw or exiv2 | exiv2 is GPL (#10). ImageIO reads the container's own EXIF/TIFF/GPS blocks, so the export carries what the camera recorded. Orientation is dropped on purpose — the geometry node already applied it, and copying the tag turns the picture twice |
| 33 | 2026-07-28 | **American spelling throughout**, code and docs | The developer's own English. `denoiseColour` is still read from old sidecars |
| 34 | 2026-07-28 | **Edits autosave: coalesced writes plus a flush on `willTerminate` and on resign-active** | `saveDevelop` ran only when switching photos, so open-one-file-edit-quit lost everything. Two triggers because they fail differently: coalescing covers the crash and the logout, `willTerminate` covers the ⌘Q that lands inside the coalescing window. The queued state travels *with* its URL, so a write cannot be misfiled during the ~50 ms a photo switch spends between `current` moving and the decode finishing |
| 35 | 2026-07-28 | **Lens corrections autoscale to keep the frame full** | poly3 pins the corners, not the edges: at k₁ = −0.35 the edge midpoints fetch 325 px past a 6024 px frame and the clamp smears the border column into a band. lensfun, darktable and Lightroom all zoom for this reason. Computed on the host in `pipe/LensGeometry.h` — it is a question about every pixel at once, which a shader cannot answer |
| 36 | 2026-07-28 | **A disabled node's consumers are told it is disabled** | `Pipeline::resolve` walks a disabled node back to the last live producer, which is correct for pixels and wrong for anything read as something other than pixels. `develop_linear` was reading the colour matrix output as log2 luminance and as guided-filter coefficients. Any node whose input carries a *meaning* rather than an image needs a flag, not just a binding |
| 37 | 2026-07-28 | **Every bench probe asserts a magnitude, against its own baseline, and gates the exit code** | "Did this change something" passed for a blacks slider delivering 39% of its effect. Floors are ratios against a reference control on the same frame, so they survive a change of photo. A known defect may be `WAIVED` with a reason string that prints every run — absent from the output is not an option |
| 38 | 2026-07-28 | **The graph's tail is eight bits for the screen and sixteen only around an export** | The drawable is `bgra8Unorm`, so `rgba16f` through the two largest nodes bought precision nothing could display, at 3.0 ms of a 16 ms budget and 383 MiB. Export widens it, reads, and narrows again. The display node dithers (ordered, Bayer 4x4) because geometry resamples its output and quantises a second time, and two roundings of a gradient is where contouring comes from. The bench asserts the two paths agree to better than one 8-bit step |
| 39 | 2026-07-28 | **Intermediates are not aliased, and that is deliberate** | Render-graph resource aliasing would cut 4.2 GiB to roughly 600 MB, but a cached node's output has to stay resident — aliasing and per-node caching are mutually exclusive. The footprint *is* the cache, and it is what makes an exposure drag 3 nodes instead of 28. The answer for smaller machines is the preview-ROI path, which shrinks every intermediate, not aliasing |
| 40 | 2026-07-28 | **Geometry stays a separate node from the display transform** | Fusing them would save ~2 ms, but `geometry.slang` resamples *display-encoded* pixels on purpose: averaging unbounded scene-linear values blooms a specular edge, which is why film and VFX resample in log rather than linear. Fusion forces scene-linear resampling and merges two small shaders into one large one, against the maintainability constraint. Revisit only if the budget gets tight — there is 6.4 ms of headroom |
| 41 | 2026-07-28 | **Grading zones are Gaussian bands on log2(Y/0.18), and the offset scales with luminance** | Linear-luma zones put middle gray at 0.70 shadows, so the shadow wheel graded the whole picture and the highlight wheel was inert. Same partition-of-unity construction as the tone bands, so a photograph has one idea of where its shadows are. Scaling the offset by Y makes a wheel a constant chromaticity shift at every exposure (0.1077 relative chroma at -3 EV against 0.1079 at +3), and makes the deep-shadow clamp that broke zero-sum unrepresentable. `k = 0.25` is derived: saturation = 1.5k/(1+k), so full travel is 30% from neutral |
| 42 | 2026-07-28 | **Bench probes gate on mean absolute per-pixel movement, not on a summary metric** | A grading wheel rotates hue at roughly constant saturation, so mean luma, mean chroma *and* mean saturation each reported a working wheel as doing nothing — three instruments, same blind spot, because a frame mean cancels a rotation. The summary is still printed for insight into *what* changed; movement decides *whether* it did. Floors are half the smaller ratio measured across two very different sample frames, because one frame was not enough — four probes calibrated on a night sky tripped on a lit forecourt |
| 43 | 2026-07-28 | **Orion is open source. Paid distribution is out of scope.** Supersedes the "undecided by choice" position in STATUS's blocked list | Settles the question that gated several others. The existing rule — avoid GPL, dynamically link LGPL — is kept anyway, because it costs nothing today and it is what keeps the decision reversible. LibRaw is dual LGPL-2.1/CDDL-1.0: taking it under CDDL permits static linking, which is the escape hatch if dynamic linking ever becomes awkward for packaging |
| 44 | 2026-07-28 | **Do not redistribute Adobe's `.dcp` profiles, and do not ship RawTherapee's either** | Adobe publishes no redistribution grant for the profiles bundled with DNG Converter, and projects that derive from them tell users to generate their own copies rather than share outputs. RawTherapee's bundled profiles are user-submitted with no stated licence of their own, inside a GPL-3 project. The DNG *specification* is separate and carries a royalty-free patent grant, so writing a DCP **reader** is fine — but any implementation distributed under that grant must carry a notice crediting DNG technology licensed from Adobe. Put it in the about box before any DNG code ships |
| 45 | 2026-07-28 | **OPEN: LibRaw's built-in colour matrices may themselves be Adobe-derived** | LibRaw's `cam_xyz` table descends from dcraw's `adobe_coeff`, whose matrices were widely understood to have been extracted from Adobe DNG Converter. If so, Orion already ships Adobe-derived colour data — the same position as darktable, RawTherapee and every other dcraw descendant, and unremarkable for an open-source project. **Evidence, checked 2026-07-28:** two independent documenters describe the table that way — Doug Pardee's ProjectDcraw notes dcraw was updated "10–14 days after Adobe releases a new version of Adobe DNG Converter", and Elle Stone's annotated dcraw source calls it "the Adobe-dng camera coefficients". Consistent, but still second-hand: no primary statement from dcraw's author was found. Stays OPEN — it would need answering before any change of distribution model, not before the next commit |
| 46 | 2026-07-28 | **A silent +1.2 EV baseline exposure, and base contrast 1.45** | Orion opened a daylight frame 1.3x darker than the camera's own JPEG, which read as flat and washed *and* pushed most of the picture into the grading shadow zone — one root, two complaints. The DNG spec's `BaselineExposure` (tag 50730) is the mechanism, applied silently on open, which is why the Exposure slider still reads 0.00. Value fitted against two independent references over six patches per frame: +1.20 EV / 1.45 is the joint best on both frames that carry signal, and mean error on the daylight frame fell 0.1543 -> 0.0194. LibRaw does not carry the tag for native ARW, so it is measured rather than read. See research/camera-profiles.md |
| 47 | 2026-07-28 | **Bench probe floors are calibrated across three frames, not one or two** | A night sky, a lit forecourt and a bright daylight cityscape. One frame tripped four probes on the second; two frames tripped six on the third — a bright frame genuinely has no deep blacks, little noise and few shadows, so those controls move less in it and that is not a regression. Half the minimum ratio over three very different pictures |
| 48 | 2026-07-28 | **The camera profile gets the DNG spec's HueSatMap stage, applied in linear ProPhoto HSV** | A 3x3 matrix is linear, so it cannot correct an error that only appears where a stimulus is saturated and narrow-band — no consumer sensor satisfies Luther-Ives. Blue sky is the worst case and Orion rendered it violet. The spec's answer is a hue/saturation table, and Orion builds one for real (90 x 25, `ValueDivisions = 1`, spec entry and loop order) rather than special-casing blue, so a `.dcp` reader later is a reader and nothing else. **`ProfileHueSatMapEncoding = 0` requires linear ProPhoto HSV**, not the working space's HSV, or the cells sit at the wrong hue angles the day a real profile loads — the node converts in and out. Table populated by a correction fitted on one hue region against two independent renderings: -8 degrees, saturation x1.05, centred at 250 degrees over a 60-degree half-width. Sky R/B 0.622 -> 0.451 against a target of 0.450. Costs one full-res pass, upstream of exposure, so the gate is unmoved at 9.37 ms p95 |
| 49 | 2026-07-28 | **Lens corrections read lensfun's database, and do not link lensfun** | The maths was never the missing part — the models were already implemented, tested and running on the GPU; what was missing was the measured numbers, and those sit in XML. Linking the library would add an LGPL-3 dependency, a build step and a second implementation of the same polynomials to obtain a value that can be read directly. The database (1,558 lenses) is vendored under `data/lensfun/` unmodified with its CC BY-SA 3.0 licence; attribution and share-alike apply to the data, not to Orion's code. The shader now evaluates ptlens, of which poly3 is the a = c = 0 case, so both of the database's distortion models land in one kernel |
| 50 | 2026-07-28 | **A confident wrong lens profile is worse than no profile**, so matching is conservative | It distorts the frame and reports that it measured it. Names normalize to letters and digits only (which collapses `f/`, case and punctuation), match by containment either way with the longest winning, and are refused below eight characters — `50mm` is inside a hundred entries and identifies none of them. A match on a differently-spelled name is flagged `approximate` and the panel says so. The developer's own Sigma 24mm F1.4 DG DN is not in the database and correctly reports nothing rather than borrowing the DSLR DG HSM entry, which is a different optical design |
| 51 | 2026-07-28 | **A profile replaces the manual sliders rather than adding to them** | Two sources for one coefficient is a control that fights the data: the profile knows what this lens does at this focal length, and a slider on top would be undoing a measurement by feel. The sliders grey out while a profile is on. Chromatic aberration stays manual in both cases — the database's TCA figures are per-copy, sample variation is the same order as the aberration, and a wrong one adds coloured edges |
| 52 | 2026-07-28 | **Exports strip GPS unless asked otherwise** | `ImageWriter` copied the whole GPS block onto every export, and the panel had no metadata control at all — a photo taken at home published the home coordinates to anyone who downloaded it, silently, with nothing in any viewer to say so. Three-way control (keep all · strip location · strip everything), defaulting to strip location, and the default is asserted in the suite because the failure mode is invisible |
| 53 | 2026-07-28 | **The generated design tokens are the palette; the hand-written mirror is deleted** | `DesignTokens.swift` is generated from `design/tokens.json` and nothing imported it — the app re-declared `Palette` by hand, with two constants renamed, one dropped and two invented. The generator also emitted Display P3 while the mirror built sRGB, so the app rendered colours the token file did not specify. Tokens are authored as sRGB hex, which is what the HTML mockup renders and where the palette was judged, so the generator emits sRGB and gains a numeric `Components` enum for the consumers that cannot take a SwiftUI `Color` (the Metal clear colour, the canvas surround) |
| 54 | 2026-07-29 | **Brush masks are reinstated into M4**, reversing the v1 cut recorded in ROADMAP and FEATURES | Asked for directly by the developer. The original cut named three costs — stroke capture, edge-aware snapping, and mask storage — and two of them turned out not to be costs. **Storage is a list of dab centres, not a raster**: a few kilobytes, resolution-independent, and exact under crop or rotation because each centre goes through the same transform the image does, where a raster mask would be 24–120 MB at 24–60 MP and compound blur every time it was resampled. **Edge-aware snapping is the guided filter Orion already has**, built and tested for dehaze — masking.md §4 is a second input binding on it, not a new algorithm. What remains is stroke capture, which is one kernel and the canvas gesture work the gradient overlay just paid for. The kernel and its tests landed the same day the decision was reversed; the interface has not. Recorded rather than quietly built, because ROADMAP and FEATURES both stated the opposite and two copies of "what we are building" disagreeing is finding #14 of the outside review |
| 55 | 2026-07-29 | **One brush node serves all four mask kinds, chained after the gradient rather than replacing it** | The graph cannot swap a node's kernel per render, and writing into a node's output from outside the graph is worse than one extra pass. Chaining works because a pass with no dabs is the identity — a property the suite already pinned, and which is now asserted over *every* pixel rather than one, because that is what the pass-through actually rests on and an off-by-one would surface at an edge. So: kind 0 gradient writes 1.0 and the brush passes it through; kinds 1 and 2 pass the gradient through; kind 3 starts from empty and lays the stroke. ⚠ The risk was never the brush — it is that every existing gradient mask now reaches `develop:linear` through a new node, so the three pre-existing measurements were re-taken and are exact: 0.4703, 0.1981, 0.4110 |
| 56 | 2026-07-29 | **Brush dab centres live outside `Adjustments`; the struct carries only a revision counter** | `Adjustments` is compared field by field on every slider tick to decide what to redirty. Carrying a stroke through that comparison would make every tick walk thousands of points. `DevelopPipeline` owns the list, set through its own facade call, and `brushRevision` — a single int — is what tells `apply` the mask is stale. The trap this creates is real and is documented at both the C and Swift boundaries: change the points without bumping the revision and the picture stops following the hand, with nothing in the code looking wrong. `Engine.setBrushStroke` therefore does both, so no caller has to remember |
| 57 | 2026-07-29 | **The mask overlay is view state, not edit state** | It is how the photographer is *looking* at the picture, not a change to it. So it is deliberately absent from `DevelopState`: it never reaches the XMP sidecar, never enters undo history, and never follows the photo to another machine. `export()` forces it off around the write and restores it afterwards, including when the export throws — an export with the overlay on would write a red-tinted photograph and nothing in the file would say why. ⚠ That guard is correct by construction but has no test |
| 58 | 2026-07-29 | **The landing site is dependency-free static files in `web/`, deployed by GitHub Actions to Pages** | React and Framer Motion were scaffolded and reverted. The page has no interactivity by explicit requirement, so React's state-to-UI core is unused, and no gestures, so Framer Motion reduces to one `IntersectionObserver`. What they would have cost is a build step, a lockfile in a C++ repository, and Vite's `base` trap — the default emits absolute asset paths that 404 on a project page **while the deploy still reports success**. That failure mode already occurred once here via `.gitignore`'s `orion-*.jpg` rule silently swallowing `web/img/*.jpg`: green push, green deploy, every image missing. Rule that came out of it: a green deploy is not proof — verify the page *and every asset* returns 200 |
| 59 | 2026-07-29 | **The landing page's static state is the finished page; JavaScript rewinds it** | The redesign adds two scroll-scrubbed scenes — the hero develops the raw as you scroll, a gradient mask sweeps a sky — and both are gated on an `html.js` class the script adds as its first act. Without JavaScript, or under `prefers-reduced-motion`, nothing is hidden and nothing is flat: the developed photograph, the placed mask and the final readout values are what the HTML itself contains. The old page had this backwards — reveal-hiding was unconditional and only JavaScript could undo it, so a no-JS visitor got a blank page |
| 60 | 2026-07-29 | **Landing page display face is Bricolage Grotesque, self-hosted in `web/fonts/`** | The page needed real display weight and the system stack cannot carry a headline at 9 rem. Self-hosted (77 KB variable woff2, latin) rather than loaded from a font service, for the same reason the app has no accounts: no third-party request, works when its owner is the only server left. Chrome discipline unchanged and now written down: teal for live numbers only, plus one new ink — film-rebate amber, from the app's own `star` token, for the frame counters that number the sections, and nothing else |
| 61 | 2026-07-29 | **The landing hero is a viewfinder, and the title is the name** | The sentence headline read as a generic hero. Now the page opens inside a camera: frame brackets, a thirds grid, an AF point that turns teal the moment the develop lands, and ORION set in Michroma — the wide engraved-on-the-body lettering cameras actually use — where the eyepiece display sits. Scrolling still develops the raw; keep going and you push through the finder, the overlay scaling past the eye while the photograph stays. The lede and the numbers moved to a strip below the hero so the finder stays clean. Michroma is self-hosted like the other two faces (11 KB) |
| 62 | 2026-07-29 | **Mask groups are one component kernel instanced per node, folding left from a written zero** | Decision #55's two-node chain (gradient + brush, one always idle) existed only because the graph cannot swap a kernel per render; a `kind` branch inside one kernel is what was actually wanted, and the merge deleted the pass-through contortion — and fixed a fourth dead control it had created (invert never reached a brush, because the gradient node held the invert and the brush node discarded its output). The group folds left in listed order per research/masking.md §6 — add is `max` (no buildup in the overlap, which is the section's whole point), subtract `α₁(1−α₂)`, intersect `α₁·α₂` — starting from a `mask:base` node that writes 0, the additive identity, so the first component needs no special case and a group of one runs the same code as a group of four. `kMaxMaskComponents` is 4: each live component is a full-res R16F pass (~46 MiB at 24 MP), so the cap is a memory number, not a concept. Compose ops and the falloff live in `ops/mask_ops.slang` — the falloff had been two identical copies each claiming to be shared. Both compose mutations (add→screen, invert-skips-brush) die |
| 63 | 2026-07-29 | **Film-rebate amber enters the app as structure only, never near the photograph** | The landing site numbers frames in amber (`star`, #F0C674) and the app had no tie to it. Amber now marks section nameplates that hold a moved control, the selected tool plate's top edge, and the histogram's clipping flags — all at the far end of the window from the canvas. It is kept off the canvas surround, off swatches and out of the colour panels, because the neutral surround exists so colour can be judged and a warm ink beside the picture biases that judgment. One ink, two roles: the rebate amber and the star rating are the same mark, the frame's own index |
| 64 | 2026-07-29 | **Version tracks the milestone; `v1.0.0` is reserved for FEATURES' v1 column** | Asked for a "v1 build"; shipped `v0.4.0-alpha.1`. Minor is the milestone in flight, so M4 closing gives 0.5.0. `FEATURES.md` still lists luminance/colour range masks, spot removal, presets, copy/paste sync, batch export and AI subject selection as v1, and a build labelled v1 promises them. The number cannot be reused; the rule can be followed by anyone |
| 65 | 2026-07-29 | **Runtime resources resolve from the bundle when there is one, and from the build tree otherwise** | `ORION_SHADER_DIR` and `ORION_DATA_DIR` are absolute paths into the source tree — correct for the tests and the bench, and fatal for anything installed: a copied app found no metallibs and died on the first open. `src/ResourcePaths.cpp` prefers `Contents/Resources` when it exists and falls back to the compile-time path, so one rule covers both and the bench needs no special casing. Verified by hiding `build/shaders` and `data/lensfun` and rendering with the packaged app — with the control that the build-tree app fails on the same run, which is what says the test was real rather than the fallback quietly working |
| 66 | 2026-07-29 | **Auto-enhance replaces the five controls it owns, and resets them before measuring** | Auto was not idempotent: press one left the frame corrected, press two measured *that* frame, derived a different look from it and solved the tone controls differently — +2.25 EV with lift 0.16, then +2.99 EV with lift 0.00. It also falsified the code's own comment, which says the look must respond to the photograph rather than to the correction; from the second press onward it responded to the correction. Auto now zeroes exposure, whites, blacks, lift and clarity before its first measurement, so it is a function of the photograph and pressing it twice is a no-op. Only those five: white balance, contrast, the crop and the curve are measured *through* and never discarded. Second cause, fixed with it: the solver ran a flat six passes, and because every step undershoots by construction (see `kDamping`) a frame far from the anchor ran out of passes short of it. It now stops when the median is within `kSettled` of the anchor, capped at 24 — the sample frames need 6, 11 and 17 |
| 67 | 2026-07-29 | **A control that changes the picture goes through `Engine.edit`, never a bare assignment** | Auto set its five sliders by direct assignment, with a comment claiming that kept them on the path an ordinary edit takes. It did not: `edit(_:_:)` is what records history, and a bare assignment records nothing — so Auto left no entry and the next undo stepped past it to the edit *before* it, appearing to discard everything the photographer had done. The rule is now explicit because the failure is invisible: the picture updates, the control shows the new value, and only the undo stack is wrong |
| 68 | 2026-07-30 | **Every piece of graph state that is not an `Adjustments` field is fanned out to both graphs by `orion::Engine`, not by the facade** | `setAdjustments` already sent the adjustment block to the full graph and the preview, and said so in bold. Nothing released them, so they sat in whatever pool enclosed the caller. Measured on `reopen` × 300, paired against an `open` × 300 control on the same folder: **+165 KB per reopen beside 200 files, +2.5 KB beside 2** — a straight line in the file count and nothing to do with the photograph. `leaks` reports zero, correctly, exactly as it did for #86: the blocks are reachable from an undrained pool. The pool goes **inside `sweep`** rather than around the caller, because the caller that shows it is the scenario runner and the caller that matters next is a batch — the function that creates O(files) temporaries is the one that should contain them. After: **+1.5 KB per reopen at 200 files, against +1.3 for the `open` control**. `testSweepDoesNotHoardTheDirectory` runs 400 sweeps of a 300-file folder in one pool and fails over 8 MB; without the pool it grows 97.8 MB |
| 91 | 2026-08-01 | **The folder index is a two-stamp cache in Application Support, and every row is validated against the file it came from** | M1's `Epic: Cull` named a SQLite index and it was never built: every folder open re-opened every raw through LibRaw, re-extracted every embedded preview and re-read every XMP. Measured at **300 frames, page cache warm: 454–688 ms cold against 28–54 ms warm, 12.9–17.2×**, and an indexless open lands on the cold number, so the win is the index and not the disk. ⚠ **The danger is not a miss, it is a hit that is wrong** — a rating nobody set, or a thumbnail of a photograph that has since been replaced, either of which looks perfectly correct on screen. So: **two stamps per row, not one.** The raw's `(mtime, size)` validates the dimensions, the camera and the thumbnail; the *sidecar's* validates the rating, the reject flag and the label. A single stamp taken from the raw is the obvious design and it is wrong, because **rating a photograph does not touch the raw** — the row would report the first rating it ever read, forever. mtime is whole **nanoseconds** from one `fstatat`, never a `Date`: pressing 4 on a photograph rated 3 rewrites the sidecar to the same length in the same second, so second granularity is a hole you can drive a cull through. The thumbnail hangs off the raw and deliberately *not* the sidecar — it is the camera's embedded preview, which no develop setting has ever affected, and invalidating it on a star press would throw away a good thumbnail sixty times a session. ⚠ **Nothing is recorded that was not read back off disk**: `refreshMarks` stats, reads, and stats again, and files nothing when the two disagree, so the contents of an old file can never be filed under the identity of a new one. `Library.persist` calls it *after* writing rather than filing what it holds in memory, because a merge that failed would otherwise leave a rating in the index that is in no sidecar. **Thumbnails are blobs in the same database**, not a folder of files: eviction and payload then move in one transaction and the cache cannot leak files whose bookkeeping is gone — decision #87's orphaned mattes, already paid for once — and it is one file to delete when a disposable cache needs disposing (Kennedy, *35% Faster Than The Filesystem*, 2017; Sears/van Ingen/Gray, MSR-TR-2006-45). **Eviction is LRU against a 512 MB budget, down to 90% of it**, because culling is folder-shaped: you finish a shoot and leave it, so least-recently-used is the folder you are done with, where LFU keeps last month's favourite and insertion order evicts the start of the folder you are still in. `used` is only rewritten when an hour stale, so opening 500 frames is 500 reads and no writes. ⚠ **A schema change drops and rebuilds; there is no migration**, because a migration implies the data is worth keeping. ⚠ **Corruption degrades, never crashes and never deletes a sidecar**: `SQLITE_CORRUPT` and `SQLITE_NOTADB` alone — never `BUSY` or `LOCKED`, which mean another process holds the file — take the index out of service, and at open discard the database once and rebuild, refusing any path not named `*.sqlite3`. This does not relitigate #79, which refused an Application Support store keyed by a path "that dies the moment the photograph moves": it refused it for **storage**. For a cache a moved photograph is a key that misses, which costs one re-read and loses nothing. SQLite is used through `import SQLite3` from the macOS SDK rather than as a dependency — it is already on every Mac, it is public domain, and the alternative was a package for something the platform ships |

So a sibling file, written on save, rename and delete and at no other time; what travels with a photograph is the raw, its sidecar, its mattes and now its versions, all caught by a `PHOTO.*` copy. What a pin cannot cover — a photograph copied without its siblings — is **reported before the version is pressed**: the row says which selections it can no longer find. ⚠ **Restoring keeps the working edit first**, as a single automatic version replaced each time, because undo alone is a trap: it is capped at fifty, coalesces, and dies with the process, while autosave overwrites the sidecar 900 ms after the restore lands. Renaming that automatic version promotes it. The engine also records one history entry, so ⌘Z takes a restore back in one step the way it takes back a preset. ⚠ **No eighth tool tab**: seven plates divide 364 points into 48 each, eight leaves 42, and VERSIONS needs about 51 at the bar's 9-point type. A vertical keystone stretches *y* and that sweep varied *x*, so the reading was taken off the axis the error is not on. What the first-order extent actually cost was invisible to it: **aspect** is `diag(1/g, g)`, exactly linear and exactly area-preserving, so √|det J| was exactly 1 and a mask stayed round while the picture under it was squeezed two to one — **0.1461 luma**, an order past any keystone reading ever recorded here. ⚠ **No fourth Rotate control**: straighten is already a rotation in the same matrix, and a second angle is two controls that do one thing and disagree about the pivot |

Two of Orion's three wheels **are** those two tints, each already carries a luminance track split toning never had, and the third grades the midtones, which split toning cannot reach. | 104 | 2026-08-01 | **Balance is a rigid shift of all three grading zone centres, ±1.25 EV at full travel, and it is not a reason to run the grade node** | The remainder #101 named when it refused split toning: the zones are Gaussians fixed at −2.5 / 0 / +2.5 EV and nothing moved them. Reference behaviour is Adobe's Balance — Split Toning from Camera Raw 4 (2007), carried into the Color Grading panel that replaced it in Camera Raw 13.0 / Lightroom Classic 10.0, October 2020 — documented as balancing the effect between the highlight and shadow ranges, positive favouring the highlights. **Rigid shift, not a re-spacing:** a partition of unity translated along its own axis is still one, and the zones keep their 2.5 EV spacing so two centres can never collide; a re-spacing squeezes the two crossovers to 1.24 EV at full deflection, which the tests now fail on. **`kBalanceEv = 1.25` is chosen** — half the zone spacing, the largest travel where a crossover stays strictly between the two centres it separates — `UNSOURCED.md` §26. **Neutral costs nothing and rebases nothing:** the shift at 0 is `−0.0f` and `x + (−0.0f) == x` bit for bit, and with the wheels centred Balance scales weights that multiply an all-zero offset and a slope of one, so it neither enables the node (#82) nor dirties the block (#92). The hole is now the part of `Ω^∪` the window fit did not recover, and "recovered" is read off the node as `rec_k > raw_k` rather than off a threshold, because demosaic ringing round a clipped edge puts pixels either side of any level constant. **Cost: 173 nodes and 7186 MiB, both unchanged** — `nRgb_` already existed and was already live, so asking for it costs a binding. ⚠ **It reopens #29 differently from #106 and owes a different argument.** #106's maximum principle does not cover this branch: `(ρ_k/ρ_j)·f_j` multiplies a rim-derived ratio by the pixel's own measured channel and *can* exceed the rim's range in level, which is the point. | 111 | 2026-08-01 | **Core ML denoise is an on-demand pass, never a graph node and never a slider — and it is not started until the gap it closes has been measured** | `research/denoise-learned.md`, written for an M5 line that had been on the roadmap since the first milestone list and never investigated. **Nothing was built; the deliverable is the write-up and `ROADMAP.md`'s piece table.** Three findings, in the order they cost. ⚠ **The domain is wrong, and not the way the line assumed.** Orion's noise *fit* is pre-demosaic (`raw/NoiseProfile.cpp`, on the `BayerImage`) but its *filter* is post-demosaic and pre-matrix, in **linear camera RGB**, because `var = a·x + b` only holds there and the color matrix would mix the variances along with the channels. Published denoisers are trained either on sRGB — gamma-encoded, tone-mapped, 8-bit, which is what SIDD's 40.30 dB measures — or on the Bayer mosaic (Brooks, Mildenhall, Xue, Chen, Sharlet & Barron, *Unprocessing Images for Learned Raw Denoising*, arXiv:1811.11127, CVPR 2019, which is the paper that names this problem and reports 14–38% lower error for fixing it). Orion's insertion point is **neither**, and an sRGB checkpoint applied there would look plausible and be wrong for a reason invisible to inspection — the purple cast's exact shape. ⚠ **It cannot be a node, and the arithmetic is one line:** at 24 Mpx one fp16 32-channel activation is **1,480 MiB, precisely what the whole existing 8-node denoise chain costs**, because 32 channels × 2 bytes and 8 nodes × 8 bytes are both 64 B/px. A four-level width-32 U-net is ~2,868 MiB for one activation per level and a guessed 4–8 GiB in practice, against a graph already holding 7,186 MiB. It tiles or it does not run. And NAFNet's own 65 GMAC at 256×256 (Chen, Chu, Zhang & Sun, *Simple Baselines for Image Restoration*, ECCV 2022, Table 6) is **48.1 TFLOP** for one 24 Mpx frame — 1.6 s even at 40 TFLOP/s sustained, **100× M0's whole 16 ms budget**. ⚠ **The blocker was never the facade.** Core ML is Objective-C — verified in this machine's SDK headers, not remembered — so it is callable from the engine's existing `.mm` files without Swift and without crossing the POD boundary; it fails by `NSError`, so the wrapper returns a status code rather than letting an exception cross and terminate the process. **Licensing is the part that actually stops it**, and decision #78's rule transfers unchanged — weights inherit their training data's terms however permissive the architecture's code is: NAFNet and Restormer are MIT, **DnCNN and FFDNet have no licence file at all** (404 from GitHub's licence API — no grant, not a permissive one; `cszn/KAIR` is the MIT route to those two), **DND is explicitly non-commercial**, and SIDD's MIT claim is a single unverified read of a project page. ⚠ **The order is the decision.** Pieces 1 and 2 are measurements, piece 3 is a written decision that is allowed to conclude "stop here", and nothing is converted or trained before them — `research/highlight-reconstruction.md`'s estimate was 16× out because the measurement came after the cost, and this is the same trap one milestone later. Guesses are labelled as guesses in both files |

JSONDecoder().decode([Preset].self)`, all or nothing, so one unreadable element left the photographer with the four built-ins and every saved look gone with nothing written anywhere to say why — and the group rename is exactly the change that would have triggered it, on every installation at once, on first launch. It decodes element-wise through `Failable` now. Element-wise `try? container.decode(...)` is the obvious alternative and is **wrong**: `UnkeyedDecodingContainer` does not promise to advance `currentIndex` on a throw, so the loop either spins forever or eats the element after the bad one depending on the Foundation build. ⚠ **`testAmericanKeyMigration` asks the shader's question, not the decoder's** — a legacy component and an American one must agree about `CanvasLayout.maskAlpha` at every point of a grid, and both must **disagree with a default component**, because a test that only proves the two agree would pass just as happily if both had quietly landed on the default |

Mutation M3 (`ctx.visibilityMoved` stuck false) was therefore **green across all nine renders** and red only in `orion-tests`: an oracle that could not fail on the one predicate governing mask enablement. One `maskhide` line fixes it and M3 now turns it red. ⚠ **Nine mutations; three are visible to the byte comparison alone** (M4, M8, M9), and M9 moves the intermediates figure 7186 → 7279 MiB — which the bench *prints* and does not assert. ⚠ **M4 found an existing check that cannot fail**: the bench's `endpointPair` turns the guided filter on with `shadows = 1e-6` and asserts the two runs agree, so hardwiring `needsGuide` false makes the "on" run the "off" run and `blacks -1, guide off/on` passes under exactly the mutation it looks built to catch. | 114 | 2026-08-02 | **X-Trans goes through LibRaw's own `xtrans_interpolate`, not a Slang port of Markesteijn — and it does not start until Markesteijn's wall clock and the white-balance question are answered** | `research/demosaic-xtrans.md`, written for an M5 line that has been on the roadmap since the first milestone list and had never been investigated. **Nothing was built; the deliverable is the write-up and `ROADMAP.md`'s seven-piece table.** ⚠ **The licensing premise the line was framed on is half right and half beside the point.** The right half: **there is no published description of Markesteijn's algorithm anywhere.** Checked dcraw (code with an attribution and no derivation), darktable's `xtrans.c` and RawTherapee's `xtransdemosaic.cc` (**GPL-3**), both projects' manuals (which say *which variant to pick*, not how either works), Fujifilm's patents (they cover the **array** — the 6×6 layout and its aperiodicity — not the interpolation, and Markesteijn is an independent contributor, not Fujifilm), and `xtransdemosaicking.blogspot.com` (the Markesteijn+FDC work that shipped in darktable 2.4.0, which calls Markesteijn as a black box). **The only extant description is source code and the accessible copies are GPL**, so re-typing one in Slang is copying rather than implementing from a description, and `CLAUDE.md` forbids it. That route is closed. ⚠ **The beside-the-point half: the same code also ships under LGPL-2.1 / CDDL-1.0.** `xtrans_interpolate(int)` is at `libraw.h:451` in LibRaw 0.22.2 — read out of this machine's installed headers, not remembered — dual-licensed by the distributed `COPYRIGHT`, and credited to *"Frank Markesteijn's algorithm"* in its own source header. The **GPL** interpolators (AMaZE, LMMSE, AFD) are in the separate, abandoned `LibRaw-demosaic-pack-GPL2/GPL3` repositories that Homebrew does not build; the X-Trans one is in the LGPL/CDDL core because it arrived through dcraw's non-RESTRICTED code. Orion already links `libraw_r` dynamically, so **nothing about the licence model changes**, and `dcraw_process()` is public with `user_qual > 2` selecting 3-pass. ⚠ **What actually makes this expensive is decision #29, not the licence.** #29 clips all three channels to a common ceiling *after* white balance and *before* demosaic, and `DevelopPipeline.cpp:1269` says so in a comment written for Bayer: moving temperature recomputes the demosaic, *"That is inherent"*. **A demosaic that leaves the GPU therefore takes the temperature slider with it**, and the four ways out are all costly — clipping post-demosaic reopens #29 and loses, a GPU preview demosaic is circular (that is the thing which does not exist licence-clean), and rescaling gains by a ratio is unsound because Markesteijn's direction decisions are not linear (`UNSOURCED.md` §29). ⚠ **Two findings that were not expected.** The GPU graph gets **smaller** — removing `linearize` and the four `rcd:*` nodes and uploading a demosaiced `RGBA16Float` source is **−5 nodes and −445.6 MiB** at 26 MP, so an X-Trans frame would run 168 nodes against a Bayer frame's 173, which is a load-immune number a bench probe can assert by name. And a 40 MP X-Trans body (X-H2 / X-T5, 7728×5152) puts the **existing** 7,186 MiB graph at **~11,800 MiB** before any of this, which is a constraint arriving with the first Fuji file whatever the demosaic is. **The published alternative is real and is not a substitute:** Rafinazari & Dubois, ICIP 2014, and Rafi Nazari's open-access uOttawa thesis (2017, ch. 3) — **opened and read**, per the rule that came out of the Tang/Tappen correction — give a frequency-domain luma-chroma method at 36.50 dB against Bayer LSLCD's 39.8 on **simulated, gamma-corrected, white-balanced** Kodak mosaics, which is not Orion's domain; and the thesis opens by naming the literature gap itself. ⚠ **It has never been compared to Markesteijn by anyone.** **The order is the decision:** pieces 2 and 3 are a measurement and a written argument, either may conclude "stop", nothing is wired before them, and **none of it starts before `DevelopPipeline.cpp` is split** — it is 2,549 lines against a ceiling of 1,000 and this feature branches the head of that graph. **Declining X-Trans, with the reason written down, is a first-class outcome** and is cheaper than a half-threaded one |

| 116 | 2026-08-02 | **The M0 gate reads the best of three rounds, not the last one, and prints every round** | The gate fed the bench's exit code off a single 60-frame sweep's p95 against 16 ms, and on this machine that is a coin toss rather than a measurement: one unchanged binary has given p95 **8.87 and 31.45 ms within the hour**, and on 2026-08-02 it failed three times running at **31.70, 22.64 and 16.52** while agents compiled in the background, then passed at **9.01** the moment the machine went quiet. ⚠ **A gate that fails for reasons the diff cannot cause is worse than no gate** — it gets read as noise, and then it is not read at all, which is how a real regression would walk through it. **Contention can only ever add time**, so across repetitions the smallest p95 is the least contaminated estimate of what the build costs, and a real regression raises the *floor* (it is in every round) while load raises the *tail* of some. Every round's p95 is printed with the spread, so a genuinely slow build cannot hide behind one lucky round and a thrashing machine is visible rather than mysterious. ⚠ **Proven still able to fail**: the threshold mutated to 5 ms gives FAIL and exit 1, restored gives PASS and exit 0 — an anti-vacuity check on a change whose whole purpose is to make a gate fail *less*. ⚠ **It narrows the sensitivity, it does not remove it**: one run still failed while three agents were compiling and three benches ran back to back, and could not be reproduced on a quiet machine, where the three rounds came in at 9.74 / 9.75 / 9.71 with a spread of 0.04 ms. **Node counts remain the load-bearing numbers**; per decision #92 each perfor mance invariant is a named-node assertion rather than a wall-clock threshold |

| 123 | 2026-08-02 | **A zero-mean error needs a spread to see it, and a solver must be started somewhere it has to work** | The last two of the three checks #118 found that cannot fail. ⚠ **`screen vs export path` compared two frame *means* and an ordered dither is zero-mean by construction**, so mutation M9a — `bayerOffset` multiplied by forty, a dither eighty times too wide — printed `ok` at -0.00313 luma against a 0.00392 bound, the gap coming only from `saturate` clipping the ends off. The fix is the **mean per-pixel deviation between the two buffers** (`meanAbsDiff`, the one instrument in the file that cannot cancel), against the same one-eight-bit-step bound stated per pixel rather than per frame. That bound is the same claim, not a new one: the narrow path is `round(v + d)` with `\|d\| <= 0.5/255`, a resample, then a second round, so the honest deviation is a fraction of a code and it scales **linearly** with the dither amplitude. Measured **0.00116 / 0.00115 / 0.00119** on `_PIC8220` / `_PIC8148` / `_PIC8095` against 0.00392 — 3.3x of headroom, and near-identical across three very different photographs because it measures the quantiser, not the picture. M9a takes it to **0.01398** (worst pixel 0.08075) and prints `DITHER MAGNITUDE`. Deterministic, not sampled-noisy: an ordered dither is a fixed table. The worst pixel is printed beside it and deliberately **not** gated — a max over 24 MP is a good thing to read and a poor thing to assert. ⚠ **`auto_enhance::refine` could be gutted with `converged` still green on `_PIC8220`** — mutation M11 — because the look sets fusion and clarity from the *first* measurement, and on that frame they land the median at 0.4570 against a 0.4610 anchor, inside `kSettled`, so the loop broke before calling `refine` once and the reported set was `+0.00 EV, +0.00 blacks, +0.00 whites`. It went red on `_PIC8095`, so the suite caught it only by luck of which file the gate command named. **The fix is to start the solve two stops away from the anchor**, in whichever direction that is for the frame — measure at rest, then displace `-2 EV` if the median is below mid-gray and `+2 EV` if above. Two stops is far outside `kSettled` under any transfer function and the response is monotonic, so the first measurement is off-anchor on *every* frame and the loop cannot exit without the solver having moved something. It is also the stronger claim, because a fixed-point iteration must not care where it started: from the displaced start `_PIC8220` converges in 8 passes to `+0.01 EV`, `_PIC8148` in 7 and `_PIC8095` in 11 from `+2.00 EV`, all landing within 0.004 of the anchor. `refines >= 1` is asserted alongside, so removing the displacement later fails loudly rather than restoring the blind spot. M11 now prints `converged: NO` and exits 1 on **all three** frames. ⚠ Both fixes are stated as node-free statistics rather than wall clocks, per #116 |

| 161 | 2026-08-03 | **Pooling and incremental re-render want the same textures, and the pool cannot simply be switched on — found by reading `render()`, before writing it** | The last step of #158 was *"free each output at its last read"*. ⚠ **It would break the interactive path.** `Pipeline::render` skips nodes that are not dirty, and a skipped node contributes **the pixels still sitting in its output texture from the previous render**. Opening a photograph is a cold render — every node runs, nothing is cached, and the 7,186 MiB is being held for a reuse that has not happened yet. That is also the only moment #152's ceiling actually bites: **the frame fails to open**, not to be edited. So the shape worth costing is a pool used for the cold render and released before the interactive one begins, or a pool restricted to nodes the dirty walk always recomputes. ⚠ **What is now certain**, and was not before this row: a single unconditional pool is the wrong answer, and it would have been found by a red M0 gate after the work was done rather than by reading `render()` for ten minutes before starting. ⚠ **#153's conclusion survives intact** — it ruled out tiling and lower precision on the grounds that the *lifetime* is the problem, and that is still true. What changes is that the lifetime worth shortening is the one *within a single cold render*, which the liveness walk already models exactly. **Nothing built.** The pool, its tests, the pins, the byte oracle and the ownership restructure are all in place and all still correct; what is missing is the answer to *when* the pool may be used, which is a measurement and a design choice rather than a patch. | 163 | 2026-08-04 | **The lens database is CC BY-SA 3.0, not GPL — refreshing it is licence-clear, and the attribution it requires was missing from a public build** | The blocker on refreshing the bundled lensfun data was *"wants a licence check before anything is downloaded"* (#144, #147). Checked: `data/lensfun/COPYING.CC_BY-SA_3.0` — **Creative Commons Attribution-ShareAlike 3.0**, which is a *data* licence and not the LGPL the lensfun **library** carries. Orion parses the XML itself in `LensDatabase.cpp` and links nothing, so the library's terms never enter, and `CLAUDE.md`'s standing caution about GPL dependencies does not bite here. **Refreshing the database is clear to do.** ⚠ **The check found a live compliance gap instead.** CC BY-SA requires attribution, and `NOTICE` had **none for the database** — its only mention of lensfun credited the *poly3 distortion model* under "algorithms implemented from published descriptions", which is a different thing entirely from redistributing ~1,450 measured calibrations. The licence text was shipping correctly (`Resources/data/lensfun/COPYING.CC_BY-SA_3.0` is inside the bundle, verified in `dist/`), so what was missing was the credit rather than the terms — the half nobody notices, in a build that is publicly downloadable. Now stated, with the project named and linked, in the `NOTICE` that ships as `Resources/licenses/NOTICE.txt`. ⚠ **Share-alike binds the data and not the application**, and the note says so because the opposite reading is the one people fear: CC BY-SA covers the database and anything derived from it, so a corrected calibration must go back under the same licence, while Orion is unaffected — as reading a CC BY-SA map does not relicense the reader. The exposure slider reads zero, reset stays correct, third-party readers honor the same tag, and there is no seed to lose. | 191 | 2026-08-24 | **HDR merge is demosaic-first, and its output is a file — an fp16 LinearRaw DNG beside the sources, not a session state** | The two structural choices of the epic, made with the developer before code. **Domain:** hdrmerge-style Bayer-domain merging keeps the result a truer raw but can only shift by whole pixels, and the requirement is handheld — rotation included — so frames are demosaiced (six-node MergeRender graph reusing the shipped linearize/RCD kernels, ~1,051 MiB at 42 MP, never the develop graph's 7 GiB, #162) and merged in scene-linear **camera-native** RGB, before white balance and before the matrix, which is exactly the state the DNG stores — so WB stays editable on re-open and the reader recovers the same fields an ARW provides. **Output:** a file, because XMP-sidecars-as-source-of-truth means everything (edits, index, batch, export) already works on files and nothing works on ephemeral sessions. fp16 because its *relative* precision puts the extra steps in the deep shadows the merge exists to improve, at half the bytes of fp32 (42 MP ≈ 253 MiB). ⚠ The GPLv3 hdrmerge clone in the tree is a citation index and is gitignored; the writer is implemented from Adobe's DNG 1.4 specification and the merge from Debevec–Malik/Hasinoff/Luijk — `research/hdr-merge.md` has the table, and NOTICE carries the DNG and OpenCV notices. | 192 | 2026-08-24 | **OpenCV is a dependency — Apache-2.0, Homebrew dylibs, confined to one translation unit** | Bracket alignment needs feature matching and robust estimation, and the mature-library rule says not to hand-roll a solved problem a solo maintainer would then own. OpenCV 5 is Apache-2.0 (license-compatible, #188's patent-grant logic applies), and the methods used are deliberately the patent-safe ones: ORB (Rublee 2011, patent-free by design), RANSAC (Fischler & Bolles 1981), ECC (Evangelidis & Psarakis 2008). ⚠ **Containment is the condition of the dependency:** `<opencv2/...>` appears in exactly one file, `engine/src/merge/Align.cpp`; `Align.h` speaks only Orion types; the CMake include path is scoped to that file with `set_source_files_properties`, so the dependency cannot leak into a header by accident. OpenCV 5 renamed its modules — features2d → features, findHomography now in geometry — recorded here because the 4.x names are what every search result says. Failure is a first-class result: too few features, a wild warp or non-convergence come back `ok = false` with an identity matrix, and the merge proceeds reference-only rather than warping garbage. | 193 | 2026-08-24 | **Opening a folder or photo from the shell is a `.zshrc` wrapper over the existing `--open` flag - no new CLI mode, and each invocation is a new instance** | The developer wants `orion` / `orion pic.ARW` from the terminal. The app side already exists: `--open` (#182) takes folders and files and walks the product's own `openFile()` path, so the whole feature is a zsh function that absolutizes paths (`open(1)` launches apps with cwd `/`) and calls `open -n -a Orion.app --args --open …`. `-n` is the deliberate part: plain `open -a` on a running app activates it and **drops `--args`** - there is no path into a running instance without an app delegate and document types, which the developer declined as out of proportion. So a second `orion` starts a second instance; XMP sidecars are the source of truth so edits are safe, though two instances may both write the SQLite index. The wrapper lives in `~/.zshrc` by request, outside the repo - the README's build section documents the flag and shows the function so it is reconstructible. |

Fix is a `runsMoved()` term comparing the flags per index; not a full `maskComponents` compare, because the guard's own discipline (#82) is to name exactly the fields the pushed struct reads, and a full compare would re-push linear params on every geometry tick. `maskmove` had the same defect shape (a swap can carry a flag across indices) and is covered by the same term. Pinned three ways: `testLayerBreakRefreshesTheLayerTable` two-pushes one pipeline (a fresh `apply` takes the `first` branch and cannot see a stale guard), `repro/mask-split-stale.txt` measures with **no set between the split and the look**, and both were watched fail on the unfixed tree. |

| 198 | 2026-08-25 | **Shift locks a corner drag to the crop's own ratio, and the maths moved to `CanvasLayout.cropDrag`** | Asked for directly: holding Shift while dragging a crop corner keeps the aspect ratio the rectangle had when the drag began, the way shape tools resize. The locked solve is: anchor at the opposite corner, dominant axis wins (`w = max(w0, h0*rho)`, the rectangle circumscribes the cursor), then an **aspect-aware** minimum (`max(w, 0.05, 0.05*rho)`) and aspect-aware frame room (`min(w, roomX, roomY*rho)`) - in that order, and the two cannot conflict because the anchor is a corner of a valid start rect. Shift is `NSEvent.modifierFlags` polled every `onChanged` tick per the `AnalogTrack`/`Filmstrip` precedent, so pressing or releasing it mid-drag snaps on the next pointer movement; everything recomputes from `startRect`, so no extra state. ⚠ **No repro scenario, deliberately**: `crop x y w h` reaches `setCrop`, never the drag layer, and adding crop getters to `controlValue` with no asserting consumer is what that function's own comment forbids. | 206 | 2026-08-28 | **Deleting a photograph means the Finder's Trash, its sidecar, versions and mattes travel with it, and nothing moves unconfirmed** | The first deletion path in the tree, so the rules are written down before the code could improvise any: (1) **Trash, never unlink** - the confirmation promises recoverability and `FileManager.trashItem` is what keeps it, including for the sidecar, so even "deleted" edits are recoverable as a set; a volume with no Trash fails into the complaint sentence rather than falling back to a hard delete. (2) **The raw moves first, per photograph** - a sidecar trashed ahead of a raw that then fails to move is a photograph that silently lost its edits, while the reverse leaves harmless orphans that are reported by name; sibling failure never stops the batch, and the outcome is one sentence, never a dialog per frame (the `Library.persist` rule). (3) **What travels is decided by `TrashPlan.plan`**, pure and listing-driven: `BASE.xmp`, `BASE.orion-snapshots.json`, `BASE.orion-matte-*.png`, each spelled by the type that owns the naming, with the `IMG_1`/`IMG_10` prefix trap pinned in `ViewportTests+Trash`. (4) **`autosave.stop()` precedes the move** when the open photograph is going - a coalesced write landing after the trash resurrects a sidecar beside a raw that is gone - and a raw that *refuses* to move re-arms exactly what was disarmed. (5) The survivors (canvas and gallery focus) are computed from the visible order before the library mutates; an emptied folder lands on the empty gallery rather than a canvas showing a photograph that is in the Trash. (6) **The index is deliberately untouched**: `plan` prunes rows absent from the next listing and a stale row misses on its stamp - the sidecars being in the Trash is the one deletion that cannot conflict with #9. Entry points: ⌘⌫ "Move to Trash…" over `cullScope`, "Delete Rejected Photos…" over the whole folder's rejects (the counted confirmation title is what keeps the folder-wide scope honest), and the gallery's context menu and header button - all through one `pendingTrash` alert in the sync-confirm shape. |

| 213 | 2026-08-14 | **The canvas decimated the render instead of averaging it — the photograph on screen was measurably noisier and harder than the same render in a file** | The developer's report, beside the one above: *"a lot noisier than Affinity and Darktable, a lot more sharp."* ⚠ **`minFilter = .linear` is not minification filtering.** It chooses between texels *within* one mip level, and the engine's output has one level, so the blit was a 2 x 2 tap however far the picture was being shrunk. Fitting 7968 x 5320 into a window is a 5x reduction: the tap reads 4 texels of every 28 and discards the rest, which is point sampling with a wide stride. Sensor noise survived at full amplitude instead of averaging down by the root of the footprint. **Measured** on the developer's frame, standard deviation of the high-pass residual, 2-tap against a true footprint average: **2.49x** in the dark background, **2.33x** in the grass, 1.61x on the shirt at a 5.31x reduction; 1.6x across the board at 2.95x. The ratio grows with the reduction, which is why a new sensor made an old defect look new. ⚠ **Display only** — the export never goes through the blit, and the exported JPEG is byte-identical before and after this change. What the photographer was comparing against Darktable was an artefact of the viewer, not of the render. ⚠ **The chain starts at half resolution, and that is a memory decision.** A full-resolution base plus levels is 226 MB at 42 Mpx and 452 MB in wide output, on the budget #162 already has the developer choosing between tiling and lower precision; halving first costs 56 MB and gives up nothing used, because the canvas only asks for the chain at a 2x reduction or more, where a half-resolution base is at worst drawn 1:1. Level 0 is one hand-written 2 x 2 average — a blit cannot scale, so level 0 *is* the copy either way. ⚠ **Only the valid rectangle is reduced**: the orientation node writes a square texture with a live top-left corner, and folding the dead remainder into the levels would fringe the picture's right and bottom edges — a defect that draws a plausible image rather than an obviously broken one. ⚠ **`generateMipmaps` was refused for the grain plate (#81)** because its filter is implementation-defined and two machines must render one edit identically; nothing here is exported, so that argument does not reach it. ⚠⚠ **`Renderer.draw` is reachable from neither suite** — the screenshot harness composites offscreen through `CanvasBlit.composite` and never calls the `MTKView` delegate — which is why this shipped and why the fix lives in `CanvasReduce.swift` rather than inside the renderer: a file with no `Engine` in it can be built by `orion-viewport-tests`. `testCanvasReductionAverages` is the assertion, and it is a one-pixel checkerboard, whose mean is mid grey and whose *every* decimation is one of two extremes: the old path scores 0 or 255, the fix scores 128. It also paints the dead region red, so folding it in is a cast rather than nothing. Gates as #201 |
| 214 | 2026-08-14 | **AgX's black latitude is 8 stops under middle gray, not the reference 10 — the default render had no toe and the shadows never reached black** | The third finding behind *"a lot noisier... and a lot more bright"*, and the only one that is a **look** rather than a defect, so it was measured, put to the developer with options, and built on their answer rather than changed unilaterally. ⚠ **Diagnosed before it was fixed, and the diagnosis is what picked the control.** Orion's export, the camera's own JPEG and macOS ImageIO's decode of one raw, patch by patch: the specular lamp agreed to **0.97x**, the ball 1.01x, the shorts 1.02x — and then grass 1.20x, shirt 1.25x, face 1.27x, darkest background **1.63x**. Agreement at the top, monotonically worse the darker the patch, **is the signature of a missing toe** and rules out every scaling explanation. Checked and ruled out individually: the camera matrix is row-normalized so white maps to white; AgX's mid gray lands at 0.497 as it should; raw saturation does reach 255 in the output; and `Engine.contrast` already opens at **1.45**, so "no contrast is applied" was not the answer either. ⚠ **The latitude rather than the slope, and that is arithmetic not taste.** Contrast in this kernel is a slope about the gray pivot, so it steepens everything: the slope that lands the background right puts the face at 0.058 against macOS's 0.085 — a face gone black to fix a background. Latitude moves only where the curve runs out, so the highlights do not move at all. **8 stops is darktable filmic rgb's own default black relative exposure**, not a fit, but it was checked by fitting: 10 / 9 / 8.5 / 8 give the darkest patch 1.63x / 1.29x / 1.13x / **0.98x**, and at 8 every patch from the lamp down lands within 10%. `research/color-pipeline.md` carries the sources and the table. ⚠⚠ **The first attempt was 1.7 stops dark across the entire picture, and the tell was the lamp.** `agxCurve` is a fit over a normalized axis on which middle gray sits at 10/16.5 = 0.606061 — **not** at the middle — so rewriting `kMinEv` while leaving `(ev - min) / (max - min)` alone slid gray to 0.498, where the polynomial returns 0.285. A toe cannot move a specular highlight, so a lamp that fell said gray had moved. The axis is normalized in **two pieces about gray** now, pinned to the polynomial's anchor, and at 10 and 6.5 stops it is identical to the expression it replaces — confirmed by rendering the developer's frame at 10 and matching the pre-change export patch for patch. ⚠⚠ **All 889 checks passed unchanged through a change that moved every shadow in the program**, which is the more useful finding: the display transform's black end had no assertion at all. `testAgxLatitudeIsAnAnchoredRescale` is four checks — middle gray renders at the sigmoid's own midpoint whatever the latitude, the declared black stop renders *exactly* zero, half a stop inside it still carries signal, and the walk down is monotone — and the mutation that restores the single-range expression fails the first at **0.387**. ⚠ **`params::Display::pivot` is retired to `pivotUnused` in the same change**: the pivot is `kPivotNorm` by construction now, and the field had been passed in **two different units** — `-2.5` (log2) from the product and `0.18` (linear) from `tests_display.cpp` — with nothing noticing, because every caller used contrast 1.0 where the pivot cancels. Gates: **893 / 3716 / check-decisions 0 / check-gestures 0 / check-wiring 0 / the three asserting scenes pass**; check-modes and check-screens still cannot run for want of samples |

| 216 | 2026-08-14 | **A rejected photograph is never written by a batch export unless it was asked for by name — the same bug, second round, opposite direction** | The developer: *"I don't want export all to export my rejected photos."* ⚠ **This is #215's feature meeting a rule that was already half-fixed.** The batch originally ignored the filter, so culling to Rated and pressing Export all wrote every reject into the folder just designated for the picks; the fix made it follow `library.targets`, which follows the filter. | 218 | 2026-09-03 | **The packaged bundle was never self-contained, and the check that said so read half the mechanism** | Found by running the packaged 0.5.0 app rather than reading it, after a month in which no release was cut. Four faults, each hiding the next. **(1)** The binary's rpath list carries `/opt/homebrew/opt/opencv/lib` *ahead of* `@executable_path/../Frameworks` (`app/CMakeLists.txt:129`), so the shipped app loaded five OpenCV dylibs out of the Cellar and never touched the copies beside them; those pulled a second `libomp` and the app died on the first raw it decoded — `OMP: Error #15 ... libomp.dylib already initialized`, one file, two paths, two OpenMP runtimes. **(2)** `package-app.sh` deleted exactly one rpath, `/opt/homebrew/opt/libraw/lib`, named when LibRaw was the only dependency; OpenCV joined the stack in `49bac4b` and nothing told the script. Now a sweep. **(3)** The dependency walk collected only absolute Homebrew paths, so `@rpath/libopencv_dnn` — how Homebrew's OpenCV names its own siblings — was invisible, and the bundle was missing 92 of its 98 libraries while appearing to work, because fault 1 caught every miss. `@loader_path` needed the library's *original* directory too, since by walk time it is a copy in `Frameworks/`. **(4)** ⚠ **The verification printed "verified no paths outside the bundle" on that bundle**: `otool -L` shows what is referenced and says nothing about where `@rpath` will look for it, so a check inspecting half the mechanism reported success for exactly the failure it exists to catch. It reads `LC_RPATH` now and the line says "and none searched". ⚠ **The cost was measured before it was argued about, and the first estimate was wrong by 4x**: `du` on `/opt/homebrew/opt/openvino/lib` says 106 MB and suggested a 300-400 MB app, but the actual dependency *closure* is **98 dylibs and 82 MB** — OpenVINO contributes 25 MB, not 106, the rest of that directory being plugins nothing references. So the alternative that was drafted — drop `opencv_video`, losing the ECC refinement `Align.cpp` already treats as best-effort inside a try/catch — buys 47 MB and was **not taken**. The bundle ships whole: **32 MB compressed**. ⚠ A fifth fault was in the fix and is worth the line: marking a library seen at *dequeue* rather than *enqueue* time made the walk quadratic, and at 98 libraries it sat at 100% CPU with no child processes for eleven minutes looking exactly like a hang. It was arithmetic. Bounded at enqueue, the same walk takes **33 seconds**. Verified: zero `/opt/homebrew` images under `DYLD_PRINT_LIBRARIES`, 13 of 13 library checks, a real render, and `--hdr-merge` — the OpenCV path itself — writing a 243 MB DNG. ⚠ Nothing published was affected: `v0.4.0-alpha.6` predates OpenCV entirely. Gates: 1012 / 4113 / all five checkers 0 |
| 219 | 2026-09-05 | **Graph textures are pooled and allocated lazily — eager allocation wanted 13.9 GiB on a 42 MP frame and took the developer's machine down with it** | Found by the machine dying, not by a gate: *"crashed my laptop from attempting to allocate memory that isn't there."* `Pipeline::compile()` gave **every one of 205 nodes its own texture, at compile time, held until the graph was destroyed** — measured at **13861 MiB** on `_PIC8095` (7968×5320). Nothing dispatched them; they were paid for whether or not a node ever coincided with the peak. Allocation is lazy now (first `computeOne`/`ensure`), a `gpu::TexturePool` recycles a texture the moment its last static reader has run, and a node whose input was recycled recomputes it — safe because every node here is a pure function of its inputs. **13861 → 1560 MiB pool high-water, 8.9x.** It is also the two-second photo swap: that allocation ran on every open. ⚠ **The A/B oracle #158 built for exactly this stopped being a tautology and passed**: 0 of 169,559,040 samples differ between two renders, which is what rules out the failure mode that matters — a pooled texture handed to the wrong reader is a *plausible photograph*, right size, right format, another node's pixels, and no mean or screenshot moves. ⚠ **The pin list grew from two to six.** #156 grepped every external reader and pinned `nFuseProxy_`/`nPeak_`, but that was before `referenceImage()` and `highlightStages()` existed; `nHueSat_`, `nRgb_`, `nHighlights_` and `nHlFill_` read a node's texture from outside the graph the same way and would have been freed at their last *in-graph* read. Re-grepped every `nodeOutput`/`referenceImage`/`highlightStages` caller; there are no others. ⚠⚠ **Lazy allocation exposed a latent read of uninitialized GPU memory, and that is the more useful find.** `highlightStages()` calls `nodeOutput(nHighlights_)`, and `highlights` is disabled whenever `highlightRecovery == 0` — the default. Eagerly it handed back a texture that had simply never been written, so the highlight-fill census and `bench_highlights` had been reading garbage all along, silently; lazily it threw and aborted `orion-tests` at `merge render: six nodes`. `nodeOutput` now steers past disabled nodes through `resolve()`, which is what every in-graph consumer already sees. ⚠ **The bench's memory block was moved after the first render and its 8 GB refusal re-pointed at `poolPeakBytes()`**: read before any render, `intermediateBytes()` reports only the source and aux plates, so the "MORE THAN AN 8 GB MAC WILL HOLD" guard was testing a number that could never reach it. `peakLiveBytes()` is printed beside the pool's own high-water mark but is **not** a threshold — it costs every node in `order_` including the disabled ones, so it over-counts and the pool lands well under it. Gates: 1012 engine checks / 0 failures |
| 220 | 2026-09-05 | **The washed-out night sky does not reproduce at the product's defaults — it reproduces at the bench's, and no constant was changed** | The developer, on their moon and star frames: *"it comes out blasted, over contrasted, and looking awful"*, *"some over saturated greenish image that looks disgusting."* The brief was that `kBaselineExposureEv`'s silent +1.2 EV (#46) lifts the deep shadows and that narrowing AgX's black latitude below 8 stops (#214) is the fix. ⚠ **Measured before anything was edited, and the premise is wrong.** At the product's own defaults — exposure 0.00 EV, `Engine.contrast` **1.45** — Orion's night sky is at or *below* macOS ImageIO's decode of the same raw on all three night frames available. The slope pivots about gray on the normalized axis, so 1.45 multiplies the distance to the black end and the shipped render reaches zero at about **5.5** stops under gray rather than at `kBlackStops`. A bench render is a look nobody is ever shown, and that is what the diagnosis was made from. ⚠ **Both controls were swept anyway, on the GPU, and #214's central claim survives**: the bright driftwood log in a daylight frame reads **0.9045 at 6, 8, 10 and 12 stops**, so the latitude really is a shape control and the highlights really do not move. Lowering the baseline lands the mean and leaves the shape (0.6 EV gives 0.3708 / 0.8442 / 0.0218). Three other daylight frames agree with both references within 4% — 0.5318 vs 0.5331/0.5460, 0.4878 vs 0.5083/0.5439, 0.5251 vs 0.5042/0.5090 — so #46's +1.2 EV is not the defect either. It now runs the same walk a second time at **1.45** and pins three numbers: gray still anchors at 0.497, four stops under gray renders **0.0175 ± 0.006** — the band a night sky lives in — and six stops is already exactly black. Mutation-tested both directions: `kBlackStops = 10`, the AgX reference latitude, fails both new checks at **0.038055** and **0.007488** (and the pre-existing black-stop check at 0.012100); `kBlackStops = 6` fails the four-stop check at **0.000217** (and the half-stop check); restoring 8 is green.
