| 221 | 2026-09-05 | **The whites slider was dead because its band sits above the brightest pixel this pipeline can produce** | The developer's report: *"The whites slider doesn't work."* **Measured** with `orion-bench` at the state the product actually opens in — not the bench's lifted one — `whites +1` moved **0.0003** where `blacks -1`, the control the same research table makes its mirror, moved 0.0189: **63x weaker**, and the smallest number in the table by a wide margin. ⚠ **The band is where the research says and the data is not.** `research/deep-research-2026-07-27.md` §3 puts whites at +5.5 EV over middle gray with an active range of +4.0 and up. `linearize` normalizes sensor saturation to 1.0 (`whiteClipFor` makes the ceiling `min(gains)`, which is the green channel's 1.0 because white balance is normalized to green) and the frame opens with `kBaselineExposureEv` = +1.2 EV applied, so the top of the scene-linear data is `2^1.2 = 2.2974`, or `log2(2.2974 / 0.18) = 1.2 + 2.473931 = **+3.674 EV**. The whole of the band's declared active range is above everything that exists; what was still working was the Gaussian's lower tail, which is why the slider was weak rather than silent and why nothing caught it. ⚠ **Ruled out individually.** The display transform: `saturate((c - kPivotNorm) * contrast + kPivotNorm)` at the product's contrast of **1.45** clips at `6.5 / 1.45 = +4.483` EV over gray, which is *above* the +3.674 ceiling, so at the default state it never reaches the data — it only bounds how far whites can push (a pixel at clip gains 1.13 EV and lands at +4.80, past it), which is a whites slider blowing a highlight and not a defect. Moving Orion's data up to meet the band instead: the +1.2 EV is a two-reference fit against Apple's RAW rendering and the camera JPEG (#190, `pipe/DevelopInternal.h`) and raising it re-breaks the midtone match it was measured to fix. ⚠ **The fix is one constant and it is no better sourced than the one it replaced**, which `research/UNSOURCED.md §1a` now says in those words. The *mismatch* is arithmetic over two sourced numbers; the *rule* that placed the band — the endpoint band goes on the axis's own white anchor — is my reading of why the source puts whites at +5.5 on an axis symmetric at ±5.5, and the source does not state it. The centers were already listed as unsourced (§1) before this change and they still are. ⚠⚠ **The bench was concealing it, and that is the other half.** The `whites +1` probe ran under a `lift` context setting `exposureEv = 4.3` — a 4.3-stop push whose only purpose could be to drag data up into a band above the sensor's clip — and asserted a floor of 0.045. A probe judged in a state no user can reach is measuring the harness. It runs at `flat` now, floor **0.016**, half the smallest of four frames: after **0.136 / 0.032 / 0.310 / 0.096** of the exposure reference, before 0.068 / 0.008 / 0.085 / 0.013. ⚠ **What the floor does not catch, said out loud.** A dead whites path reads 0.000 and fails everywhere — verified by zeroing the term in `applyTone`, which turns the probe NO EFFECT with `orion-bench` exit 1 and breaks two checks in `testLocalAdjustments`, while the neighbouring `whites +1, guide off/on` invariant still prints `ok` because it renders both sides through the same dead code. But the band being *mis-placed* is only caught where the picture has highlights: the two night frames fell under 0.016 before the fix and `samples/_PIC8095` did not, at 0.068, and a floor high enough to trip on it would trip on a night frame's correct 0.032. There is no single number that does both. ⚠ The sample symlinks all point at moon photographs, so three of the four frames had to come from `~/Pictures` by hand; a highlight control cannot be calibrated on a near-black frame with a small bright subject. ⚠ **Blacks was left at −5.5 although the same argument puts it at −8.0** (`kBlackStops`, where the AgX toe runs out of latitude, #214): it measurably reaches real data where it is, it is not the reported bug, and moving it would be tuning with nothing behind it. Whites and highlights are 1.17 EV apart now against the source's 3.0, so they overlap far more than intended; what still separates them is that highlights reads the guided-filter neighbourhood and whites reads the pixel, which is the distinction the research actually argues for. ⚠ `tests_linear.cpp` carries the same center in closed form and moved with it — a re-derivation by a different route is not an independent oracle for the constants themselves, only for the construction. Gates: **1012 checks / 0 failures**, check-decisions clean, every control probe green; the bench's M0 latency gate reads 15.50–17.02 ms across six runs on this machine both before and after the change and is unrelated |
| 222 | 2026-09-06 | **The shipping contrast hard-clips real shadow detail 2.5 EV inside the declared latitude, and no contrast value fixes it without giving up the punch #46 fitted for** | The developer, repeatedly, on a daylight frame: *"the RAW doesn't render anything close to the JPEG. it comes out blasted, over contrasted, and looking awful"* — a person in `DSC09734`'s bottom-left corner disappears into flat black the camera JPEG still shows with texture. Mechanism (already traced): `develop_display.slang:238`'s `saturate((c - kPivotNorm) * contrast + kPivotNorm)` hard-clips the normalized log axis *before* the AgX sigmoid, at `-kBlackStops/contrast` stops under gray — `-5.517` at the shipping 1.45, 2.5 EV shallower than the declared 8-stop latitude. **7 frames** (`~/Pictures/sept 5th forks/`, deep shade/open daylight/backlit, shadow+midtone+highlight patch each) against the camera JPEG and macOS ImageIO (`research/color-pipeline.md` carries the table). ⚠ **A mean-luma fit — the method #46/#220/#221 used — is the wrong instrument and actively misleads**: swept 0.8-2.0, aggregate error against macOS falls monotonically *past* 1.45 (0.0878 at 1.0, 0.0615 at 1.45, 0.0516 at 2.0) because the same slope that clips shadows is also what gives highlights punch (they sit under the +3.674 EV data ceiling per #221, so it's the slope and not a clamp there), and a coarse mean cannot distinguish "got darker correctly" from "got clipped." **What does see it**: near-black(<2/255)/exact-(0,0,0) fraction of a real GPU-rendered patch (`--scenario`'s `shot`, not the bench) — DSC09734 0.01%/0% at contrast 1.0 rising to 20.73%/0.07% at 1.45; DSC09765 1.94%/0% to 62.62%/26.14%; DSC09783 0.71%/0.04% to 91.82%/36.01% — against the camera JPEG's 0% exact-black on all three and macOS's 0/1.30/2.47%, so Orion produces 15-25x more literal `(0,0,0)` pixels than macOS's own (darker but soft-toed) rendering on the same patch. The crush grows continuously from 1.0 with no plateau, so no compromise contrast is defensible: low enough to hold shadows (~1.0-1.05) surrenders essentially all the midtone/highlight punch #46 fitted 1.45 for, reproducing the "flat and washed" complaint #46 was answering. `kBaselineExposureEv` was not touched and could not have helped — #220 already established a baseline shift moves *which* EVs land in the clipped zone, not whether the clamp is hard. **`Engine.contrast` stays 1.45**, its doc comment (`app/Engine.swift:129-142`) now describes the actual defect instead of a superseded 1.15 fit; a soft roll-off is specified in `research/color-pipeline.md` (replace the hard `saturate` with a monotonic injective compressive function, re-derive `agxCurve`'s effective zero crossing since its own constant term is negative, a bit-identical-output test) but not built — `develop_display.slang` is outside this session's file ownership and this is shader-design work, not a constant tune. `testAgxLatitudeIsAnAnchoredRescale` (`apps/tests/tests_display.cpp`) extended with two checks pinning the contrast-scaled clip boundary itself (`kClipEv ± {0.5, 0.15}` EV, asymmetric because `agxCurve`'s own zero crossing sits ~0.46 EV past the pre-curve saturate's, not at it); mutation-tested by setting the shader's `kBlackStops` to 10, which fails both new checks (0.010689 where 0.0 was wanted) alongside the two pre-existing ones (#220), and restoring 8 is green.
